                    $N_\varnothing$.}
                \label{fig:chordal_anti_4}
            \end{figure}
    \end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}
    Let $G$ be a connected chordal $P_5$-free graph with and induced copy of
    $2K_2$ and free of the graphs depicted in
    \Cref{fig:chordal-p5-free-obstructions}. Let $X$ and $Y$ be different proper
    subsets of $Z$. Suppose that $N_Z=\l{z}$.
    \begin{lemenum}
        \item If $N_X$ and $N_Y$ are 1-bags adjacent to different edges of the
            $2K_2$, then at least one of $N_X$ and $N_Y$ is empty.
            \label{lem:chordal_types_5}
        \item If $N_X$ is a 2-bag complete to an edge of the $2K_2$, then
            $N_X \cap N(z)$ is a clique. \label{lem:chordal_types_K_clique}
        \item The set $G[N_\varnothing \cap N(z)]$ is a cluster.
            \label{lem:chordal_types_K_cluster}
        \item If $N_X$ is a 1-bag, then $N_X$ is stable.
            \label{lem:chordal_types_4}
        \item If $N_X$ is a 2-bag complete to an edge of the $2K_2$, then
            non-isolated vertices of $G[N_\varnothing \setminus N(z)]$ are
            anticomplete to $N_X \cap N(z)$.
            \label{lem:chordal_types_trivial}
        \item If $N_X$ is a 2-bag complete to an edge of the $2K_2$ and $N_Y$ is
            a 1-bag adjacent to the other edge of the $2K_2$, then at least one
            of $N_{X} \setminus N(z)$ and $N_Y$ is empty.
            \label{lem:chordal_types_empty}
    \end{lemenum}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
    \leavevmode \par
    \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
        \item Suppose without loss of generality that $X=\l{a}$ and $Y=\l{c}$.
            If $x\in N_X$ and $y\in N_Y$, by \Cref{lem:chordal_anti_1}, we have that
            $z$ is nonadjacent to both $x$ and $y$. Then, $G$ has an induced copy of
            either $C_5$ or $P_5$, depending on whether $x$ is adjacent to $y$ or
            not, respectively (see \Cref{fig:chordal_types_5}). Hence, one between
            $N_X$ and $N_Y$ is empty.

            \begin{figure}[htb!]
                \centering
                \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node distance=1cm,
                    on grid, edge]
                    \begin{scope}[xshift=-2cm]
                        \node (z) {};
                        \node[above left=1 and 0.75 of z] (a) {};
                        \node[above right=1 and 0.75 of z, lightgray] (b) {};
                        \node[below left=1 and 0.75 of z] (c) {};
                        \node[below right=1 and 0.75 of z, lightgray] (d) {};
                        \node[above left=0.5 and 0.75 of z] (x) {};
                        \node[below left=0.5 and 0.75 of z] (y) {};
        
                        \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
                        \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (z) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (z) to (d);
                        \draw (x) to (a);
                        \draw (z) to (a);
                        \draw (y) to (c);
                        \draw (x) to (y);
                        \draw (z) to (c);
                    \end{scope}

                    \begin{scope}[xshift=2cm]
                        \node (z) {};
                        \node[above left=1 and 0.75 of z] (a) {};
                        \node[above right=1 and 0.75 of z, lightgray] (b) {};
                        \node[below left=1 and 0.75 of z] (c) {};
                        \node[below right=1 and 0.75 of z, lightgray] (d) {};
                        \node[above left=0.5 and 0.75 of z] (x) {};
                        \node[below left=0.5 and 0.75 of z] (y) {};
        
                        \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
                        \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (z) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (z) to (d);
                        \draw (x) to (a);
                        \draw (z) to (a);
                        \draw (y) to (c);
                        \draw (z) to (c);
                    \end{scope}
                \end{tikzpicture}
                \caption{An induced copy of $C_5$ ($\p{x,a,z,c,y,x}$) or $P_5$
                    ($\p{x,a,z,c,y}$) given that both $N_X$ and $N_Y$ are
                    nonempty.}
                \label{fig:chordal_types_5}
            \end{figure}

        \item Suppose without loss of generality that $X=\l{a,b}$. If there are
            nonadjacent vertices $x$ and $y$ in $N_X\cap N(z)$, notice
            $\l{a,c,d,x,y,z}$ induces a copy of $H_4$ (see
            \Cref{fig:chordal_types_K_clique}). Therefore, $N_X\cap N(z)$ is a
            clique.

            \begin{figure}[htb!]
                \centering
                \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
                    distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
                    \node (v) {};
                    \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (a) {};
                    \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (b) {};
                    \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (c) {};
                    \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (d) {};
                    \node[above=1.5cm of v] (x) {};
                    \node[left=0.75cm of v] (y) {};

                    \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
                    \draw[lightgray] (x) to (b);
                    \draw[lightgray] (y) to (b);
                    \draw[lightgray] (v) to (b);
                    \draw (x) to (a);
                    \draw (y) to (a);
                    \draw (c) to (d);
                    \draw (y) to (v);
                    \draw (x) to (v);
                    \draw (v) to (a);
                    \draw (v) to (c);
                    \draw (v) to (d);
                \end{tikzpicture}
                \caption{An induced copy of $H_4$ given that $N_X\cap N(z)$ is
                    not a clique.}
                \label{fig:chordal_types_K_clique}
            \end{figure}

        \item Suppose $G[N_\varnothing \cap N(z)]$ is not a cluster, so there is
            an induced copy of $P_3$, $\p{w,x,y}$, with $w,x,y\in N_\varnothing
            \cap N(z)$. Then $\l{a,b,w,x,y,z}$ induces a copy of $H_4$ (see
            \Cref{fig:chordal_types_K_cluster}). It follows that
            $G[N_\varnothing \cap N(z)]$ is a cluster.

            \begin{figure}[htb!]
                \centering
                \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
                    distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
                    \node (v) {};
                    \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (a) {};
                    \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (b) {};
                    \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (c) {};
                    \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (d) {};
                    \node (x) at (-30:1) {};
                    \node (y) at (0:1) {};
                    \node (z) at (30:1) {};
                    \node[Label] at ($(0,0) - (y)$) {};

                    \draw[lightgray] (v) to (c);
                    \draw[lightgray] (v) to (d);
                    \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
                    \draw (a) to (b);
                    \draw (v) to (b);
                    \draw (v) to (a);
                    \draw (v) to (x);
                    \draw (v) to (y);
                    \draw (v) to (z);
                    \draw (x) to (y);
                    \draw (z) to (y);
                \end{tikzpicture}
                \caption{An induced copy of $H_4$ given that $G[N_\varnothing
                    \cap N(z)]$ is not a cluster.}
                \label{fig:chordal_types_K_cluster}
            \end{figure}

        \item Suppose without loss of generality that $X=\l{a}$. Let $x,x'\in
            N_X$. By \Cref{lem:chordal_anti_1}, $z$ is nonadjacent to both $x$
            and $x'$. If $x$ and $x'$ are adjacent, then $\l{a,b,c,d,x,x',z}$
            induces a copy of $H_6$ (see \Cref{fig:chordal_types_4}). We
            conclude that $N_X$ is stable.
            
            \begin{figure}[htb!]
                \centering
                \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
                    distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
                    \node (z) {};
                    \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of z] (a) {};
                    \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of z] (b) {};
                    \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of z] (c) {};
                    \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of z] (d) {};
                    \node[above=1cm of a] (x) {};
                    \node[right=0.75cm of x] (x') {};

                    \draw (z) to (c);
                    \draw (z) to (d);
                    \draw (c) to (d);
                    \draw (a) to (b);
                    \draw (z) to (b);
                    \draw (z) to (a);
                    \draw (a) to (x);
                    \draw (a) to (x');
                    \draw (x) to (x');
                \end{tikzpicture}
                \caption{An induced copy of $H_6$ given that $N_X$ is not stable.}
                \label{fig:chordal_types_4}
            \end{figure}

        \item Suppose without loss of generality that $X=\l{a,b}$. Let $w$ be a
            non-isolated vertex of $G[N_\varnothing \setminus N(z)]$. Then,
            there exists a vertex $w'$ of $G[N_\varnothing \setminus N(z)]$ that
            is adjacent to $w$. If $w$ is adjacent to $x\in N_X\cap N(z)$, then
            $G$ contains an induced copy of either $H_6$ or $P_5$, depending on
            whether $w'$ is adjacent to $x$ or not, respectively (see
            \Cref{fig:chordal_types_trivial}). It follows that non-isolated
            vertices of $G[N_\varnothing \setminus N(z)]$ are anticomplete to
            $N_X \cap N(z)$.

            \begin{figure}[htb!]
                \centering
                \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
                    distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
                    \begin{scope}[xshift=-2cm]
                        \node (v) {};
                        \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (a) {};
                        \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (b) {};
                        \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (c) {};
                        \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (d) {};
                        \node (x) at (0,1.5) {};
                        \node (y) at (0.75,2) {};
                        \node (z) at (-0.75,2) {};

                        \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (x) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (v) to (b);
                        \draw (v) to (a);
                        \draw (x) to (a);
                        \draw (v) to (c);
                        \draw (v) to (d);
                        \draw (c) to (d);
                        \draw (x) to (z);
                        \draw (x) to (v);
                        \draw (x) to (y);
                        \draw (z) to (y);
                    \end{scope}

                    \begin{scope}[xshift=2cm]
                        \node (v) {};
                        \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (a) {};
                        \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (b) {};
                        \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (c) {};
                        \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (d) {};
                        \node (x) at (0,1.5) {};
                        \node (y) at (0.75,2) {};
                        \node (z) at (-0.75,2) {};

                        \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (x) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (v) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (v) to (a);
                        \draw[lightgray] (x) to (a);
                        \draw[lightgray] (v) to (d);
                        \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
                        \draw (v) to (c);
                        \draw (x) to (v);
                        \draw (x) to (y);
                        \draw (z) to (y);
                    \end{scope}
                \end{tikzpicture}
                \caption{An induced copy of $H_6$ (induced by
                    $\l{a,c,d,w,w',x,z}$) or $P_5$ ($\p{w',w,x,z,c}$) given that
                    non-isolated vertices of $G[N_\varnothing \setminus N(z)]$
                    are not anticomplete to $N_X\cap N(z)$.}
                \label{fig:chordal_types_trivial}
            \end{figure}

        \item Suppose without loss of generality that $X=\l{a,b}$ and $Y=\l{c}$.
            Let $x\in N_x\setminus N(z)$ and $y\in N_Y$. By
            \Cref{lem:chordal_anti_4}, $y$ is not adajcent to $z$. Then, $G$
            contains either an induced copy of $C_5$ or $P_5$ depending on
            whether $x$ is adjacent to $y$ or not, respectively (see
            \Cref{fig:chordal_types_empty}). Therefore, one between
            $N_X\setminus N(z)$ and $N_Y$ is empty.

            \begin{figure}[htb!]
                \centering
                \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node distance=1cm,
                    on grid, edge]
                    \begin{scope}[xshift=-2cm]
                        \node (z) {};
                        \node[above left=1 and 0.75 of z] (a) {};
                        \node[above right=1 and 0.75 of z, lightgray] (b) {};
                        \node[below left=1 and 0.75 of z] (c) {};
                        \node[below right=1 and 0.75 of z, lightgray] (d) {};
                        \node[above left=0.5 and 0.75 of z] (x) {};
                        \node[below left=0.5 and 0.75 of z] (y) {};
        
                        \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
                        \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (z) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (x) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (z) to (d);
                        \draw (x) to (a);
                        \draw (z) to (a);
                        \draw (y) to (c);
                        \draw (x) to (y);
                        \draw (z) to (c);
                    \end{scope}

                    \begin{scope}[xshift=2cm]
                        \node (z) {};
                        \node[above left=1 and 0.75 of z] (a) {};
                        \node[above right=1 and 0.75 of z, lightgray] (b) {};
                        \node[below left=1 and 0.75 of z] (c) {};
                        \node[below right=1 and 0.75 of z, lightgray] (d) {};
                        \node[above left=0.5 and 0.75 of z] (x) {};
                        \node[below left=0.5 and 0.75 of z] (y) {};
        
                        \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
                        \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (z) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (z) to (d);
                        \draw[lightgray] (x) to (b);
                        \draw (x) to (a);
                        \draw (z) to (a);
                        \draw (y) to (c);
                        \draw (z) to (c);
                    \end{scope}
                \end{tikzpicture}
                \caption{An induced copy of $C_5$ ($\p{x,a,z,c,y,x}$) or $P_5$
                    ($\p{x,a,z,c,y}$) given that both $N_X\setminus N(z)$ and
                    $N_Y$ are nonempty.}
                \label{fig:chordal_types_empty}
            \end{figure}

    \end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}
    \label{lem:neighborhoods_chordal_comp}
    Let $G$ be a connected chordal $P_5$-free graph with and induced copy of
    $2K_2$ and free of the graphs depicted in
    \Cref{fig:chordal-p5-free-obstructions}. Let $X$ and $Y$ be different proper
    subsets of $Z$. Suppose that $N_Z=\l{z}$.
    \begin{lemenum}
        \item If $N_X$ and $N_Y$ are 2-bags complete to different edges of the
            $2K_2$, then at least one of $N_X$ and $N_Y$ is complete to $z$.
            \label{lem:chordal_comp_1}
        \item If $N_X$ is a 2-bag complete to an edge of the $2K_2$, then
            non-isolated vertices of $G[N_X\setminus N(z)]$ are complete to
            $N_X\cap N(z)$. \label{lem:chordal_comp_2}
    \end{lemenum}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
    \leavevmode \par
    \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
        \item Suppose without loss of generality that $X=\l{a,b}$ and
            $Y=\l{c,d}$. Suppose $x\in N_X$ and $y\in N_Y$ are not adjacent to
            $z$. By \Cref{lem:chordal_anti_3}, $x$ is not adjacent to $y$. Then,
            $\p{x,a,z,c,y}$ is an induced copy of $P_5$ (see
            \Cref{fig:chordal_comp_1}). It follows that one between $N_X$ and
            $N_Y$ is complete to $z$.

            \begin{figure}[htb!]
                \centering
                \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node distance=1cm,
                    on grid, edge]
                    \node (z) {};
                    \node[above left=1 and 0.75 of z] (a) {};
                    \node[above right=1 and 0.75 of z, lightgray] (b) {};
                    \node[below left=1 and 0.75 of z] (c) {};
                    \node[below right=1 and 0.75 of z, lightgray] (d) {};
                    \node[above left=0.5 and 0.75 of z] (x) {};
                    \node[below left=0.5 and 0.75 of z] (y) {};
    
                    \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
                    \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
                    \draw[lightgray] (z) to (b);
                    \draw[lightgray] (z) to (d);
                    \draw[lightgray] (x) to (b);
                    \draw[lightgray] (y) to (d);
                    \draw (x) to (a);
                    \draw (z) to (a);
                    \draw (y) to (c);
                    \draw (z) to (c);
                \end{tikzpicture}
                \caption{An induced copy of $P_5$ given that both $N_X$ and
                    $N_Y$ are not complete to $z$.}
                \label{fig:chordal_comp_1}
            \end{figure}

        \item Suppose without loss of generality that $X=\l{a,b}$. Let $x$ be a
            non-isolated vertex of $G[N_X\setminus N(z)]$. Then, there exists a
            vertex $x'$ of $G[N_X\setminus N(z)]$ that is adjacent to $x$. If
            $w\in N_X\cap N(z)$ is not adjacent to both $x$ and $x'$, then $G$
            contains an induced copy of $P_5$ or $H_1$, depending on whether one
            or none of $x$ and $x'$ are adjacent to $w$ (see
            \Cref{fig:chordal_comp_2}). Thus, non-isolated vertices of
            $G[N_X\setminus N(z)]$ are complete to $N_X\cap N(z)$.

            \begin{figure}[htb!]
                \centering
                \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
                    distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
                    \begin{scope}[xshift=-2cm]
                        \node (v) {};
                        \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (a) {};
                        \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (b) {};
                        \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (c) {};
                        \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (d) {};
                        \node (x) at (-0.5,1.5) {};
                        \node (y) at (0.5,1.5) {};
                        \node[left=0.75cm of v] (w) {};
    
                        \draw (a) to (b);
                        \draw (x) to (a);
                        \draw (x) to (b);
                        \draw (y) to (a);
                        \draw (y) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
                        \draw (w) to (v);
                        \draw (w) to (a);
                        \draw (w) to (b);
                        \draw (x) to (y);
                        \draw (v) to (a);
                        \draw (v) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (v) to (c);
                        \draw[lightgray] (v) to (d);
                    \end{scope}

                    \begin{scope}[xshift=2cm]
                        \node (v) {};
                        \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (a) {};
                        \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (b) {};
                        \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (c) {};
                        \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (d) {};
                        \node (x) at (-0.5,1.5) {};
                        \node (y) at (0.5,1.5) {};
                        \node[left=0.75cm of v] (w) {};

                        \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (x) to (a);
                        \draw[lightgray] (x) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (y) to (a);
                        \draw[lightgray] (y) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (w) to (a);
                        \draw[lightgray] (w) to (b);
                        \draw[lightgray] (v) to (d);
                        \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
                        \draw[lightgray] (v) to (a);
                        \draw[lightgray] (v) to (b);
                        \draw (y) to (w);
                        \draw (w) to (v);
                        \draw (x) to (y);
                        \draw (v) to (c);
                    \end{scope}
                \end{tikzpicture}
                \caption{An induced copy of $H_1$ (induced by
                    $\l{a,b,w,x,x',z}$) or $P_5$ ($\p{x,x',w,z,c}$) given that
                    non-isolated vertices of $G[N_X\setminus N(z)]$ are not
                    complete to $N_X\cap N(z)$.}
                \label{fig:chordal_comp_2}
            \end{figure}
    \end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}
    \label{thm:monopolar_chordal}
    A connected chordal $(P_5,\text{house})$-free graph $G$ with an induced copy
    of $2K_2$ is monopolar if and only if it does not contain any of the graphs
    depicted in \Cref{fig:chordal-p5-free-obstructions} as an induced subgraph.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
    Recall that $Z$ is the vertex set of the initial induced copy of $2K_2$.
    Since monopolarity is hereditary, it follows from
    \Cref{prop:minimal_obs_chordal} that $G$ does not contain any of the
    mentioned graphs as an induced subgraph.

    Suppose that $G$ does not contain any of the graphs depicted in
    \Cref{fig:chordal-p5-free-obstructions}. We proceed by considering the
    following cases:

    \paragraph{Case 1: $N_Z$ is nonempty.} By \Cref{lem:chordal_types_1}, $N_Z$
    has exactly one vertex, namely $z$. Additionally, \Cref{lem:chordal_types_3}
    implies that no other bag is adjacent to vertices of both edges of the
    $2K_2$. Also, by \Cref{lem:chordal_types_5}, suppose without loss of
    generality that both $N_c$ and $N_d$ are empty.

    Let $K$ be the set of vertices of $N_{ab}$ that are complete to $N_Z$. By
    \Cref{lem:chordal_types_K_clique}, $K$ is a clique. Similarly, the set of
    vertices in $N_{cd}$ that are complete to $N_Z$, $K'$, is a clique.
    Furthermore, $K$ and $K'$ are anticomplete to each other by
    \Cref{lem:chordal_anti_3}. Then,
    \[
        K\cup \l{a,b} \cup K' \cup \l{c,d}
    \]
    induces a $2$-cluster. Now let $\mathcal{C}$ be the set of connected
    components in $G[N_\varnothing]$. In particular, let $\mathcal{C}_z$ be the
    set of connected components in $\mathcal{C}$ that are adjacent to $z$. For
    each $C\in \mathcal{C}_z$, let $C_z$ be the graph induced by the vertices of
    $C$ that are adjacent to $z$. Notice \Cref{lem:chordal_types_K_cluster}
    implies that $C_z$ is a cluster. Also, \Cref{lem:chordal_anti_2_bag} ensures
    that $C_z$ is anticomplete to $K$ and $K'$, so
    \[
        \bigcup_{C\in \mathcal{C}_z} V_{C_z} \cup K\cup \l{a,b} \cup K' 
        \cup \l{c,d}
    \]
    induces a cluster.
    
    Meanwhile, because of \Cref{lem:chordal_types_4}, we have that $N_a$ and
    $N_b$ are stable, while \Cref{lem:chordal_anti_2} implies that they are
    anticomplete to each other. Moreover, \Cref{lem:chordal_anti_1} ensures that
    both $N_a$ and $N_b$ are anticomplete to $z$, so
    \[
        N_a \cup N_b \cup \l{z}
    \]
    is stable. Observe that each component $C\in \mathcal{C}\setminus
    \mathcal{C}_z$ is anticomplete to $z$. Additionally,
    \Cref{lem:chordal_anti_4} implies that $V_C$ is anticomplete to both $N_a$
    and $N_b$.  Since $G$ is connected, $V_C$ must be adjacent to the set
    $N_{ab} \cup N_{cd}$. Due to \Cref{lem:chordal_anti_5}, $V_C$ can only be
    adjacent to either $K$ or $K'$. Nonetheless,
    \Cref{lem:chordal_types_trivial} implies that $C$ is the trivial graph, so
    \[
        \bigcup_{C\in\mathcal{C}\setminus \mathcal{C}_z} V_C \cup N_a \cup 
        N_b \cup \l{z}
    \]
    is stable. We still have to decide how to arrange the following sets:
    \begin{itemize}
        \item $N_{ab}\setminus K$,
        \item $N_{cd}\setminus K'$, and
        \item sets $V_{C-C_z}$ for $C \in \mathcal{C}_z$.
    \end{itemize}
    We proceed by taking subcases:

    \paragraph{Case 1.1: $N_{ab}$ is not complete to $z$.} By
    \Cref{lem:chordal_comp_1}, $N_{cd}$ is complete to $z$, so in fact, $N_{cd}
    = K'$. 
    
    We claim that, for each $C\in \mathcal{C}_z$, $V_{C-C_z}$ is empty. Indeed,
    since $N_{ab}\setminus K$ is nonempty, let $x\in N_{ab}\setminus K$. If
    $V_{C - C_z}$ is nonempty, since $C$ is connected, there are adjacent
    vertices $w\in V_{C - C_z}$ and $w'\in V_{C_z}$. By
    \Cref{lem:chordal_anti_5}, $x$ and $y$ are anticomplete to $w$, so
    $\p{w,w',z,a,x}$ is a $P_5$ (see \Cref{fig:empty_component}). Thus,
    $V_{C-C_z}$ is empty.

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
            distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
            \node (z) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of z] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of z, lightgray] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of z, lightgray] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of z, lightgray] (d) {};
            \node (w) at (0.75,-0.5) {};
            \node (w') at (0.75,0.5) {};
            \node (x) at (0,1.5) {};

            \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (z) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (z) to (c);
            \draw[lightgray] (z) to (d);
            \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
            \draw[lightgray] (x) to (b);
            \draw (a) to (x);
            \draw (z) to (a);
            \draw (w') to (z);
            \draw (w') to (w);
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{An induced copy of $P_5$ given that $V_{C-C_z}$ is nonempty.}
        \label{fig:empty_component}
    \end{figure}
    
    By \Cref{lem:chordal_anti_5}, $N_{ab}\setminus K$ is anticomplete to $V_C$
    for $C\in \mathcal{C}$. Furthermore, \Cref{lem:chordal_anti_2_bag_2} implies
    that $N_{ab}\setminus K$ is anticomplete to both $N_a$ and $N_b$. Once more,
    we proceed by taking the following subcases:
    
    \paragraph{Case 1.1.1: $N_{ab}\setminus K$ is stable.} Aforementioned
    arguments imply that
    \[
        \bigcup_{C\in\mathcal{C}\setminus \mathcal{C}_z} V_C \cup 
        \p{N_{ab}\setminus K} \cup N_a \cup N_b \cup \l{z}
    \]
    is stable, so
    \[
        \p{\bigcup_{C\in\mathcal{C}\setminus \mathcal{C}_z} V_C \cup 
        \p{N_{ab}\setminus K} \cup N_a \cup N_b \cup \l{z} , 
        \bigcup_{C\in \mathcal{C}_z} V_{C_z} \cup K\cup \l{a,b} \cup K' 
        \cup \l{c,d}}
    \]
    is a monopolar partition of $G$  (see
    \Cref{fig:monopolar_partition_chordal_1}).

    \begin{figure}[htb!]    
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
            distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
            \node (v) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (d) {};
            \node (cd) at (0,-1.5) {};
            \node (ab1) at (0,1.5) {};
            \node (ab2) at ($(b) + (-0.25,1.5)$) {};
            \node (ab3) at ($(a) + (0.25,1.5)$) {};
            \node (a') at ($(a) + (120:1)$) {};
            \node (b') at ($(b) + (60:1)$) {};
            \node (v1) at ($(v) + (1,0)$) {};
            \node (v2) at ($(v1) + (30:0.75)$) {};
            \node (v3) at ($(v1) + (-30:0.75)$) {};
            \node (e) at (0,3) {};
            \node [Label] at ($(0,0) - (v2)$) {};

            \draw (a) to (b);
            \draw (v) to (a);
            \draw (v) to (b);
            \draw (v) to (c);
            \draw (v) to (d);
            \draw (c) to (d);
            \draw (c) to (cd);
            \draw (d) to (cd);
            \draw (v) to (cd);
            \draw (a) to (ab1);
            \draw (b) to (ab1);
            \draw (v) to (ab1);
            \draw (a) to (ab2);
            \draw (b) to (ab2);
            \draw (a) to (ab3);
            \draw (b) to (ab3);
            \draw (ab2) to (ab1);
            \draw (a') to (a);
            \draw (b') to (b);
            \draw (a') to (ab1);
            \draw (v) to (v1);
            \draw (v) to (v2);
            \draw (v) to (v3);
            \draw (v1) to (v2);
            \draw (v1) to (v3);
            \draw (v2) to (v3);
            \draw (ab1) to (e);

            \begin{scope}[on background layer, scale=1.5]
                \draw[fill=red!20, draw=none] \convexpath{c,d,cd}{3.5pt};
                \draw[fill=red!20, draw=none] \convexpath{v1,v2,v3}{3.5pt};
                \draw[fill=red!20, draw=none] \convexpath{ab1, b, a}{3.5pt};
                \draw[fill=blue!20, draw=none] \convexpath{ab2, ab3}{3.5pt};
                \node[fit= (v), inner sep=0.5pt, draw=none, fill=blue!20] {};
                \node[fit= (a'), inner sep=0.5pt, draw=none, fill=blue!20] {};
                \node[fit= (b'), inner sep=0.5pt, draw=none, fill=blue!20] {};
                \node[fit= (e), inner sep=0.5pt, draw=none, fill=blue!20] {};
            \end{scope}
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{A monopolar partition of $G$.}
        \label{fig:monopolar_partition_chordal_1}
    \end{figure}

    \paragraph{Case 1.1.2: $N_{ab}\setminus K$ is not stable.} Notice $G[N_{ab}
    \setminus K]$ is split by \Cref{lem:chordal_types_6}, so let
    $(S_{ab},K_{ab})$ be a split partition of it, where $K_{ab}$ is a maximal
    clique. As in the previous subcase, we already have that
    \[
        \bigcup_{C\in\mathcal{C}\setminus \mathcal{C}_z} V_C \cup S_{ab} \cup
        N_a \cup N_b \cup \l{z}
    \]
    is stable. Furthermore, since $N_{ab}\setminus K$ is not stable and $K_{ab}$
    is maximal, it follows that $K_{ab}$ contains at least two vertices.
    Therefore, by \Cref{lem:chordal_comp_2}, $K_{ab}$ is complete to $K$.
    Additionally, \Cref{lem:chordal_anti_3} implies that $K_{ab}$ is
    anticomplete to $K'$. Since we have already stated that $N_{ab}\setminus K$
    is anticomplete to $V_C$ for $C\in \mathcal{C}$, we have that
    \[
        \bigcup_{C\in \mathcal{C}_z} V_{C_z} \cup K \cup K_{ab} \cup 
        \l{a,b} \cup K' \cup \l{c,d}
    \]
    induces a cluster, so
    \[
        \p{\bigcup_{C\in\mathcal{C}\setminus \mathcal{C}_z} V_C \cup 
        S_{ab} \cup N_a \cup N_b \cup \l{z} , 
        \bigcup_{C\in \mathcal{C}_z} V_{C_z} \cup K \cup K_{ab}\cup
        \l{a,b} \cup K' \cup \l{c,d}}
    \]
    is a monopolar partition of $G$ (see
    \Cref{fig:monopolar_partition_chordal_2}).

    \begin{figure}[htb!]    
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
            distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
            \node (v) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (d) {};
            \node (cd) at (0,-1.5) {};
            \node (ab1) at (0,2.5) {};
            \node (ab2) at ($(ab1) + (30:0.75)$) {};
            \node (ab3) at ($(ab1) + (150:0.75)$) {};
            \node (ab4) at ($(a) + (105:1)$) {};
            \node (ab5) at ($(b) + (75:1)$) {};
            \node (a') at ($(a) + (135:1.5)$) {};
            \node (b') at ($(b) + (45:1.5)$) {};
            \node (v1) at ($(v) + (1,0)$) {};
            \node (v2) at ($(v1) + (30:0.75)$) {};
            \node (v3) at ($(v1) + (-30:0.75)$) {};
            \node (ab6) at ($(ab3) + (30:0.75)$) {};
            \node[right=1.5 of ab1] (e) {};

            \draw (a) to (b);
            \draw (v) to (a);
            \draw (v) to (b);
            \draw (v) to (c);
            \draw (v) to (d);
            \draw (c) to (d);
            \draw (c) to (cd);
            \draw (d) to (cd);
            \draw (v) to (cd);
            \draw (a) to (ab1);
            \draw (b) to (ab1);
            \draw (v) to (ab1);
            \draw (a) to (ab2);
            \draw (b) to (ab2);
            \draw (a) to (ab3);
            \draw (b) to (ab3);
            \draw (ab2) to (ab1);
            \draw (a') to (a);
            \draw (b') to (b);
            \draw (a') to (ab1);
            \draw (v) to (v1);
            \draw (v) to (v2);
            \draw (v) to (v3);
            \draw (v1) to (v2);
            \draw (v1) to (v3);
            \draw (v2) to (v3);
            \draw (ab4) to (a);
            \draw (ab5) to (a);
            \draw (ab4) to (b);
            \draw (ab5) to (b);
            \draw (ab5) to (ab4);
            \draw (ab5) to (ab1);
            \draw (ab4) to (ab1);
            \draw (ab3) to (ab4);
            \draw (ab3) to (ab1);
            \draw (ab2) to (ab5);
            \draw (ab6) to (a);
            \draw (ab6) to (b);
            \draw (ab1) to (e);

            \begin{scope}[on background layer, scale=1.5]
                \draw[fill=red!20, draw=none] \convexpath{c,d,cd}{3.5pt};
                \draw[fill=red!20, draw=none] \convexpath{v1,v2,v3}{3.5pt};
                \draw[fill=red!20, draw=none] \convexpath{ab1, ab5, b, a, 
                    ab4}{3.5pt};
                \draw[fill=blue!20, draw=none] \convexpath{ab2, ab3, ab6}
                    {3.5pt};
                \node[fit= (v), inner sep=0.5pt, draw=none, fill=blue!20] {};
                \node[fit= (a'), inner sep=0.5pt, draw=none, fill=blue!20] {};
                \node[fit= (b'), inner sep=0.5pt, draw=none, fill=blue!20] {};
                \node[fit= (e), inner sep=0.5pt, draw=none, fill=blue!20] {};
            \end{scope}
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{A monopolar partition of $G$.}
        \label{fig:monopolar_partition_chordal_2}
    \end{figure}

    \paragraph{Case 1.2: $N_{cd}$ is not complete to $z$.} Once more, due to
    \Cref{lem:chordal_comp_1}, $N_{ab}=K$. Additionally, $N_{cd}\setminus K'$
    being nonempty implies, by \Cref{lem:chordal_types_empty}, that both $N_a$
    and $N_b$ are empty. Furthermore, as in the previous case, we still have
    that $N_{cd}\setminus K$ being nonempty implies that, for each $C\in
    \mathcal{C}_z$, $V_{C-C_z}$ is empty.
    
    Also, every claim from the previous subcase regarding $N_{ab}$ still hold in
    this case for $N_{cd}$, since every lemma used works for any 2-bag complete
    to an edge of an induced copy of $2K_2$. Hence, if $N_{cd}\setminus K'$ is
    stable, then
    \[
        \p{\bigcup_{C\in\mathcal{C}\setminus \mathcal{C}_z} V_C \cup 
        \p{N_{cd}\setminus K'} \cup \l{z} , \bigcup_{C\in \mathcal{C}_z}
        V_{C_z} \cup K\cup \l{a,b} \cup K' \cup \l{c,d}}
    \]
    is a monopolar partition of $G$, whereas if $N_{cd}\setminus K'$ is not
    stable, then we use its split partition $(S_{cd},K_{cd})$ with $K_{cd}$ a
    maximal clique, to obtain that
    \[
        \p{\bigcup_{C\in\mathcal{C}\setminus \mathcal{C}_z} V_C \cup 
        S_{cd} \cup \l{z} , \bigcup_{C\in \mathcal{C}_z} V_{C_z} \cup K \cup
        \l{a,b} \cup K' \cup K_{cd} \cup \l{c,d}}
    \]
    is a monopolar partition of $G$.

    \paragraph{Case 1.3: Both $N_{ab}$ and $N_{cd}$ are complete to $z$.} It is
    immediate that $N_{ab}=K$ and $N_{cd}=K'$, so we only need to check how to
    partition the sets $V_{C-C_z}$ for each $C\in \mathcal{C}_z$.
    
    Let $\mathcal{C}_{C,0}$ be the set of connected components of $C-C_z$ that
    are trivial. Similarly, let $\mathcal{C}_{C,1}$ be the set of connected
    components of $C-C_z$ that are not trivial. Recall that, by
    \Cref{lem:chordal_anti_4}, $V_C$ is anticomplete to both $N_a$ and $N_b$.
    Thus,
    \[
        \bigcup_{C\in \mathcal{C} \setminus \mathcal{C}_z} V_C \cup 
        \bigcup_{C\in \mathcal{C}_z} \p{\bigcup_{D\in \mathcal{C}_{C,0}} V_D} 
        \cup N_a \cup N_b \cup \l{z}
    \]
    is stable. Let $D\in \mathcal{C}_{C,1}$. Since $C$ is connected, there is a
    connected component of $C_z$, $D_z$, to which $D$ is adjacent. Let $x\in
    V_{D}$ and $y\in V_{D_z}$ be adjacent vertices. If there is a vertex $w\in
    V_{C_z}$ not adjacent to $y$ and adjacent to $x$, then $\p{w,x,y,z,w}$ is a
    $C_4$ (see \Cref{fig:more_than_one_component}).

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
            distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
            \node (v) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (d) {};
            \node (x) at (30:1.25) {};
            \node (w) at (-30:1.25) {};
            \node (y) at ($(x) + (-30:1.25)$) {};
            \node[Label] at ($(0,0) - (y)$) {};

            \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (v) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (v) to (c);
            \draw[lightgray] (v) to (d);
            \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
            \draw[lightgray] (v) to (a);
            \draw (v) to (w);
            \draw (v) to (x);
            \draw (y) to (x);
            \draw (y) to (w);
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{An induced copy of $C_4$ given that $D$ is adjacent to more
        than one component of $C_z$.}
        \label{fig:more_than_one_component}
    \end{figure}
    
    It follows that $D_z$ is the unique connected component of $C_z$ to which
    $D$ is adjacent to. Furthermore, we claim that $D$ is complete to $D_z$.
    Indeed, let $x$ be a vertex of $D$ adjacent to a vertex $y$ of $D_z$. Since
    $D$ is nontrivial, there is a vertex $w$ of $D$ adjacent to $x$. Notice that
    $w$ must be adjacent to $y$, otherwise, $\p{w,x,y,z,a}$ is a $P_5$ (see
    \Cref{fig:z_nonadjacent_y}).
    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
            distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
            \node (v) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (d) {};
            \node (y) at (0.75,0) {};
            \node (x) at ($(y) + (30:0.75)$) {};
            \node (w) at ($(y) + (-30:0.75)$) {};
            \node[Label] at ($(0,0) - (w)$) {};

            \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (v) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (v) to (c);
            \draw[lightgray] (v) to (d);
            \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
            \draw (v) to (a);
            \draw (x) to (w);
            \draw (y) to (v);
            \draw (y) to (w);
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{An induced copy of $P_5$ given that $w$ is not adjacent to
        $y$.}
        \label{fig:z_nonadjacent_y}
    \end{figure}
    
    \noindent
    Since $D$ is connected, it follows that $D$ is complete to $y$. Moreover, if
    a vertex $y'$ of $D_z$ is adjacent to $y$, the aforementioned argument
    implies that either $D$ is complete or anticomplete to $y'$. Nonetheless, if
    the latter case happens, then $\l{c,d,w,x,y,y',z}$ induces a copy of $H_6$
    (see \Cref{fig:D_complete_y}). Therefore, $D$ is complete to $D_z$.

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
            distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
            \node (v) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (d) {};
            \node (x) at ($(v) + (30:1)$) {};
            \node (w) at ($(v) + (-30:1)$) {};
            \node (y) at ($(w) + (30:1)$) {};
            \node (z) at ($(w) + (-30:1)$) {};
            \node[Label] at ($(0,0) - (y)$) {};

            \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (v) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (v) to (a);
            \draw (v) to (c);
            \draw (v) to (d);
            \draw (c) to (d);
            \draw (x) to (w);
            \draw (x) to (v);
            \draw (v) to (w);
            \draw (y) to (w);
            \draw (y) to (z);
            \draw (z) to (w);
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{An induced copy of $H_6$ given that $D$ is anticomplete to
        $y'$.}
        \label{fig:D_complete_y}
    \end{figure}

    Finally, we claim that $D$ is the unique component in $\mathcal{C}_{C,1}$.
    If there was another component, $D'$, the two previous claims imply that
    there is a unique component $D_z'$ of $C_z$ to which $D'$ is complete. Since
    $D'$ is nontrivial and connected, there are adjacent vertices $x'$ and $w'$
    of $D'$. Similarly, let $x$ and $w$ be adjacent vertices of $D$. On the one
    hand, if $D_z$ happens to be $D_z'$, then if $y$ is a vertex of $D_z$, it
    follows that $\l{a,b,z,c,d,y,x,w,x',w'}$ induces a copy of $H_5$ (see
    \Cref{fig:D_unique_1}).

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
            distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
            \node (v) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (d) {};
            \node (w) at (2,0) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of w] (y') {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of w] (y) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of w] (x) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of w] (x') {};
            \node[Label] at ($(0,0)-(x')$){};

            \draw (a) to (b);
            \draw (v) to (b);
            \draw (v) to (c);
            \draw (v) to (d);
            \draw (c) to (d);
            \draw (v) to (a);
            \draw (x) to (w);
            \draw (y) to (w);
            \draw (y') to (w);
            \draw (x') to (w);
            \draw (x) to (x');
            \draw (y) to (y');
            \draw (v) to (w);
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{An induced copy of $H_5$ given that $D$ is not unique.}
        \label{fig:D_unique_1}
    \end{figure}

    \noindent
    On the other hand, let $y$ be a vertex of $D_z$ and $y'$ be a vertex of
    $D_z'$. Recall that $C_z$ is a cluster, so $y$ and $y'$ are nonadjacent. In
    addition to it, since $D$ is anticomplete to all components in $C_z$ but
    $D_z$, and $D'$ is anticomplete to all components in $C_z$ but $D_z'$, we
    have that $x$ is anticomplete to $y'$ and $x'$ is anticomplete to $y$. Thus,
    $\p{x,y,z,y',x'}$ is an induced copy of $P_5$ (see \Cref{fig:D_unique_2}).
    We conclude that $D$ is unique.

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
            distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
            \node (v) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (d) {};
            \node (w) at (2,0.5) {};
            \node (w') at (2,-0.5) {};
            \node[above right=0.5cm and 0.75cm of w, lightgray] (y') {};
            \node[above left=0.5cm and 0.75cm of w] (y) {};
            \node[below left=0.5cm and 0.75cm of w'] (x) {};
            \node[below right=0.5cm and 0.75cm of w', lightgray] (x') {};
            \node[Label] at ($(0,0)-(x')$){};

            \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (v) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (v) to (c);
            \draw[lightgray] (v) to (d);
            \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
            \draw[lightgray] (v) to (a);
            \draw[lightgray] (x') to (w');
            \draw[lightgray] (y') to (w);
            \draw[lightgray] (x) to (x');
            \draw[lightgray] (y) to (y');
            \draw (x) to (w');
            \draw (y) to (w);
            \draw (v) to (w);
            \draw (v) to (w');
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{An induced copy of $P_5$ given that $D$ is not unique.}
        \label{fig:D_unique_2}
    \end{figure}

    Furthermore, we claim that $G[D]$ is split. If that was not the case, there
    is a $2K_2$ in $D$. Let $w$ and $x$ be adjacent vertices of $D$ and
    anticomplete to adjacent vertices $w'$ and $x'$ of $D$. If $y$ is a vertex
    of $D_z$, then we have that $\l{a,b,z,c,d,y,w,x,w',x'}$ induces a copy of
    $H_5$ (see \Cref{fig:C-C_v_split}).
    
    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
            distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
            \node (v) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (d) {};
            \node (w) at (2,0) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of w] (y') {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of w] (y) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of w] (x) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of w] (x') {};
            \node[Label] at ($(0,0)-(x')$){};

            \draw (a) to (b);
            \draw (v) to (b);
            \draw (v) to (c);
            \draw (v) to (d);
            \draw (c) to (d);
            \draw (v) to (a);
            \draw (x) to (w);
            \draw (y) to (w);
            \draw (y') to (w);
            \draw (x') to (w);
            \draw (x) to (x');
            \draw (y) to (y');
            \draw (v) to (w);
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{An induced copy of $H_5$ given that a component in
        $\mathcal{C}_{C,1}$ is not split.}
        \label{fig:C-C_v_split}
    \end{figure}

    Thus, $G[D]$ is split, so let $(S_D,K_D)$ be a split partition of $V_D$ such
    that $K_D$ is a maximal clique. Recall that $D$ is complete to $D_z$, so
    $K_D \cup V_{D_z}$ is a clique. Moreover, since $D$ is anticomplete to all
    other connected components of $C_z$, it follows that
    \begin{align*}
        \Bigg(& \bigcup_{C\in \mathcal{C}\setminus \mathcal{C}_z} V_C \cup 
        \bigcup_{C\in \mathcal{C}_z} \p{\bigcup_{D\in \mathcal{C}_{C,0}} V_D
        \cup \bigcup_{D\in \mathcal{C}_{C,1}} S_D} \cup N_a \cup  N_b \cup
        \l{z}, \\ 
        & \bigcup_{C\in \mathcal{C}_z} \p{V_{C_z} \cup 
        \bigcup_{D\in \mathcal{C}_{C,1}} K_D} \cup 
        K \cup \l{a,b} \cup K' \cup \l{c,d} \Bigg)
    \end{align*}
    is a monopolar partition of $G$ (see
    \Cref{fig:monopolar_partition_chordal_3})

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
            distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
            \node (v) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (d) {};
            \node (cd) at (0,-1.5) {};
            \node (ab1) at (0,1.5) {};
            \node (v1) at ($(v) - (1,0)$) {};
            \node (v2) at ($(v1) - (30:0.75)$) {};
            \node (v3) at ($(v1) - (-30:0.75)$) {};

            \begin{scope}[xshift=2cm, yshift=-0.5cm, rotate=15]
                \foreach \i in {0,...,4}
                    \node (\i)  at ({(360/5)*\i + 90}:0.75){};
                
                \foreach \i in {0,...,4}{
                    \foreach \j in {1,...,4}{
                        \draw let \n1={int(mod(\i+\j,5))} in (\i) to (\n1);
                    }
                }

                \node (23) at (0,-1) {};

                \draw (23) to (0);
                \draw (23) to (4);
                \draw (23) to (1);
                \draw (23) to (3);
            \end{scope}

            \path let \p1=(1) in node (1') at (\x1, 0.75) {};
            \path let \p1=(0) in node (0') at (\x1, 0.75) {};
            \path let \p1=(4) in node (4') at (\x1, 0.75) {};
            \node [Label] at ($(0,0) - (4)$) {};

            \draw (a) to (b);
            \draw (v) to (a);
            \draw (v) to (b);
            \draw (v) to (c);
            \draw (v) to (d);
            \draw (c) to (d);
            \draw (c) to (cd);
            \draw (d) to (cd);
            \draw (v) to (cd);
            \draw (a) to (ab1);
            \draw (b) to (ab1);
            \draw (v) to (ab1);
            \draw (v) to (v1);
            \draw (v) to (v2);
            \draw (v) to (v3);
            \draw (v1) to (v2);
            \draw (v1) to (v3);
            \draw (v2) to (v3);
            \draw (v) to (0);
            \draw (v) to (1);
            \draw (v) to (4);
            \draw (1) to (1');
            \draw (1) to (0');
            \draw (0) to (0');
            \draw (0) to (4');
            \draw (4) to (4');

            \begin{scope}[on background layer, scale=1.5]
                \draw[fill=red!20, draw=none] \convexpath{c,d,cd}{3.5pt};
                \draw[fill=red!20, draw=none] \convexpath{v1,v2,v3}{3.5pt};
                \draw[fill=blue!20, draw=none] \convexpath{1',0',4'}{3.5pt};
                \draw[fill=red!20, draw=none] \convexpath{ab1, b, a}{3.5pt};
                \draw[fill=red!20, draw=none] \convexpath{0,4,3,2,1}{3.5pt};
                \node[fit= (v), inner sep=0.5pt, draw=none, fill=blue!20] {};
                \node[fit= (23), inner sep=0.5pt, draw=none, fill=blue!20] {};
            \end{scope}
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{A monopolar partition of $G$.}
        \label{fig:monopolar_partition_chordal_3}
    \end{figure}
    
    \paragraph{Case 2: $N_Z$ is empty.} Right from the start, consider the
    following subcases:

    \paragraph{Case 2.1: At least one 3-bag is nonempty.} Suppose without loss
    of generality that $N_{abc}$ is nonempty and let $x$ be a vertex in
    $N_{abc}$. By \Cref{lem:chordal_types_pending}, we have that $N_d$ is empty.
    Moreover, by \Cref{lem:chordal_types_3}, all other bags adjacent to both
    edges of the $2K_2$ are empty. Therefore, the only other bags that can be
    nonempty are:
    \[
        N_a, N_b, N_c, N_{ab}, N_{cd} \text{ and } N_{\varnothing}.
    \]
    
    If $y\in N_c$ is adjacent to $x$, then $\l{a,b,c,y}$ induces a copy of
    $2K_2$ and $x$ is complete to such a set, so one may proceed as in the
    previous case (see \Cref{fig:3_bag_1}).

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node distance=1cm,
            on grid, edge]
            \node (x) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of x] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of x] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of x] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of x, lightgray] (d) {};
            \node [left=0.75 of x] (y) {};

            \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
            \draw (x) to (c);
            \draw (a) to (b);
            \draw (x) to (a);
            \draw (x) to (b);
            \draw (x) to (y);
            \draw (y) to (c);
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{An induced copy of $2K_2$ with a vertex complete to it.}
        \label{fig:3_bag_1}
    \end{figure}

    Therefore, consider $N_c$ anticomplete to $N_{abc}$. Similarly, $N_{cd}$ is
    anticomplete to $N_{abc}$. Meanwhile, we claim that $N_{a}$ is complete to
    $N_{abc}$. Indeed, if $y\in N_a$ is not adjacent to $x$, then
    $\p{y,a,x,c,d}$ is a $P_5$ (see \Cref{fig:3_bag_2}).

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node distance=1cm,
            on grid, edge]
            \node (x) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of x] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of x, lightgray] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of x] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of x] (d) {};
            \node [left=0.75 of x] (y) {};

            \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (x) to (b);
            \draw (c) to (d);
            \draw (x) to (c);
            \draw (x) to (a);
            \draw (a) to (y);
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{An induced copy $P_5$ given that $N_a$ is not complete to
            $N_{abc}$.}
        \label{fig:3_bag_2}
    \end{figure}
    
    \noindent
    It follows that $N_a$ is complete to $N_{abc}$. Similarly, we get that
    $N_{ab}$ and $N_b$ is complete to $N_{abc}$. This in turn implies that any
    bag (different from $N_{abc}$) adjacent to the edge $ab$ is anticomplete to
    any bag adjacent to the edge $cd$ (also different from $N_{abc}$),
    otherwise, there would be a $C_4$ (see \Cref{fig:3_bag_3}).

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node distance=1cm,
            on grid, edge]
            \node (x) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of x, lightgray] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of x, lightgray] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of x] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of x, lightgray] (d) {};
            \node[right=0.75 of x] (y) {};
            \node[below = 0.75 of x] (w) {};

            \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (x) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
            \draw[lightgray] (w) to (d);
            \draw[lightgray] (b) to (y);
            \draw[lightgray] (x) to (a);
            \draw (x) to (c);
            \draw (y) to (x);
            \draw (w) to (c);
            \draw (w) to (y);
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{An induced copy of $C_4$ given that $N_{b}$ is not anticomplete
        to $N_{cd}$.}
        \label{fig:3_bag_3}
    \end{figure}

    Furthermore, for a vertex $y\in N_\varnothing$, if $y$ is adjacent to $w$ in
    $N_{cd}$, either $\p{y,w,c,x,y}$ is a $C_4$, or $\p{y,w,c,x,a}$ is a $P_5$,
    depending on whether $x$ is adjacent to $w$ or not, respectively (see
    \Cref{fig:3_bag_4}).

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node distance=1cm,
            on grid, edge]
            \begin{scope}[xshift=-2cm]
                \node (x) {};
                \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of x, lightgray] (a) {};
                \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of x, lightgray] (b) {};
                \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of x] (c) {};
                \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of x, lightgray] (d) {};
                \node[right=0.75 of x] (y) {};
                \node[below = 0.75 of x] (w) {};

                \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
                \draw[lightgray] (x) to (b);
                \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
                \draw[lightgray] (w) to (d);
                \draw[lightgray] (x) to (a);
                \draw (x) to (c);
                \draw (y) to (x);
                \draw (w) to (c);
                \draw (w) to (y);
            \end{scope}

            \begin{scope}[xshift=2cm]
                \node (x) {};
                \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of x, lightgray] (a) {};
                \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of x] (b) {};
                \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of x] (c) {};
                \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of x, lightgray] (d) {};
                \node[right=0.75 of x] (y) {};
                \node[below = 0.75 of x] (w) {};

                \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
                \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
                \draw[lightgray] (w) to (d);
                \draw[lightgray] (x) to (a);
                \draw (x) to (b);
                \draw (x) to (c);
                \draw (w) to (c);
                \draw (w) to (y);
            \end{scope}
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{An induced copy $C_4$ or $P_5$ given that $N_\varnothing$ is
            not anticomplete to $N_{cd}$.}
        \label{fig:3_bag_4}
    \end{figure}

    It follows that $N_\varnothing$ is anticomplete to $N_{cd}$, and similarly
    follows that $N_\varnothing$ is anticomplete to $N_{c}$.

    We claim that $N_\varnothing$ is complete to $N_{abc}$. Indeed, since $G$ is
    connected, each connected component of $N_\varnothing$ is adjacent to
    another bag. By the last argument, each connected component of
    $N_\varnothing$ can only be adjacent to $N_{a}$, $N_{b}$, $N_{ab}$ or
    $N_{abc}$. If $y$ in $N_\varnothing$ is adjacent to a vertex $w$ in $N_{ab}$
    and $y$ is not adjacent to $x$, then, since $w$ is adjacent to $x$, we have
    that $\p{y,w,x,c,d}$ is an induced copy of $P_5$ (see \Cref{fig:3_bag_5}).

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node distance=1cm,
            on grid, edge]
            \node (x) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of x, lightgray] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of x, lightgray] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of x] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of x] (d) {};
            \node (ab) at (0,1.5) {};
            \node [right=0.75 of ab] (y) {};

            \draw[lightgray] (x) to (a);
            \draw[lightgray] (x) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (ab) to (a);
            \draw[lightgray] (ab) to (b);
            \draw (ab) to (x);
            \draw (c) to (d);
            \draw (x) to (c);
            \draw (ab) to (y);
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{An induced copy of $P_5$ given that $y$ is not adjacent to
        $x$.}
        \label{fig:3_bag_5}
    \end{figure}

    Hence, $x$ is adjacent to $y$. Since $x$ is arbitrary and $N_{abc}$ is
    complete to $N_{ab}$, it follows that $y$ is complete to $N_{abc}$.
    Moreover, if $y'$ in $N_\varnothing$ is adjacent to $y$, then $y'$ must be
    adjacent to $x$ as well, otherwise, $\p{y',y,x,c,d}$ is an induced copy of
    $P_5$ (see \Cref{fig:3_bag_6}).

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
            distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
            \node (v) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (d) {};
            \node (x) at ($(v) + (0.75,0.5)$) {};
            \node (w) at ($(v) + (0.75,-0.5)$) {};

            \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (v) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (v) to (a);
            \draw (c) to (d);
            \draw (v) to (c);
            \draw (x) to (w);
            \draw (x) to (v);
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{An induced copy of $P_5$ given that $y'$ is not adjacent to 
        $x$.}
        \label{fig:3_bag_6}
    \end{figure}

    \noindent
    Therefore, the connected component of $y$ in $G[N_\varnothing]$ is complete
    to $N_{abc}$. The same argument works for connected components of
    $N_\varnothing$ that are adjacent to either $N_a$ or $N_b$. Thus,
    $N_\varnothing$ is complete to $N_{abc}$.

    By \Cref{lem:chordal_types_clique_bag}, $N_{abc}$ is a clique. Thus, we have
    proven that every vertex lies in exactly one of the sets $N(c)$ and $N(x)$.
    Moreover, we have also shown that $N(x)\setminus \l{c}$ and $N(c)\setminus
    \l{x}$ happen to be anticomplete to each other. This implies that at least
    one of $G[N(c)\setminus \l{x}]$ and $G[N(x)\setminus\l{c}]$ is a cluster,
    otherwise, $G$ contains an induced copy of $H_7$ (see \Cref{fig:3_bag_7}).

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
            distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
            \node (v) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (d) {};
            \node (x) at (-0.75,0) {};
            \node (w) at (0,-1.75) {};
            \node (y) at (-0.75,-1.75) {};

            \draw (a) to (b);
            \draw (x) to (a);
            \draw (v) to (b);
            \draw (v) to (a);
            \draw (c) to (d);
            \draw (v) to (c);
            \draw (c) to (w);
            \draw (d) to (w);
            \draw (x) to (v);
            \draw (y) to (w);
            \draw (y) to (c);
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{An induced copy of $H_7$ given that both
            $G[N(x)\setminus\l{c}]$ and $G[N(c)\setminus\l{x}]$ are not
            clusters.}
        \label{fig:3_bag_7}
    \end{figure}

    Also notice that we can consider both $G[N(x)\setminus\l{c}]$ and
    $G[N(c)\setminus\l{x}]$ to be split, otherwise, one of them contains an
    induced copy of $2K_2$ and, such a copy of $2K_2$ along with $x$ or $c$
    would reduce this case to Case 1. We take the following subcases:

    \paragraph{Case 2.1.1: $G[N(c)\setminus \l{x}]$ is a cluster.} Since
    $G[N(x)\setminus\l{c}]$ is split, let $(S_x,K_x)$ be a split partition of
    it, with $S_x$ a stable set and $K_x$ a clique. Recall that $N(x)\setminus
    \l{c}$ is anticomplete to $c$ and $N(c)\setminus \l{x}$ is anticomplete to
    $(N(x)\setminus\l{c})\cup \l{x}$. Then,
    \[
        \p{S_x \cup \l{c} , K_x \cup \l{x} \cup (N(c)\setminus \l{x})}
    \]
    is a monopolar partition of $G$ (see
    \Cref{fig:monopolar_partition_chordal_4}).

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
            distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
            \node[Label] (v) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (d) {};
            \node (z1) at (-0.5,0) {};
            \node (z2) at (0.5,0) {};
            \node (ab1) at (-0.5,1.75) {};
            \node (ab2) at (0.5,1.75) {};
            \node[left=0.75 of a] (e) {};
            \node[right=0.75 of b] (b') {};
            \node[below=0.5 of e] (a') {};
            \node[below=0.5 of b'] (b'') {};
            \node (c') at ($(c) + (255:0.75)$) {};
            \node (c'') at ($(c) + (195:0.75)$) {};
            \node (cd1) at (-0.5, -1.75) {};
            \node (cd2) at (0.5, -1.75) {};

            \draw (z1) to (a);
            \draw (z1) to (b);
            \draw (z1) to (c);
            \draw (z2) to (a);
            \draw (z2) to (b);
            \draw (z2) to (c);
            \draw (z1) to (z2);
            \draw (a) to (b);
            \draw (c) to (d);
            \draw (ab1) to (a);
            \draw (ab1) to (b);
            \draw (ab1) to (z1);
            \draw (ab1) to (z2);
            \draw (ab2) to (a);
            \draw (ab2) to (b);
            \draw (ab2) to (z1);
            \draw (ab2) to (z2);
            \draw (e) to (z1);
            \draw (e) to (z2);
            \draw (e) to (ab1);
            \draw (a) to (a');
            \draw (z1) to (a');
            \draw (z2) to (a');
            \draw (b') to (b);
            \draw (b') to (ab1);
            \draw (b'') to (b);
            \draw (b'') to (z1);
            \draw (b'') to (z2);
            \draw (b') to (z1);
            \draw (b') to (z2);
            \draw (c') to (c);
            \draw (c'') to (c);
            \draw (cd1) to (c);
            \draw (cd1) to (d);
            \draw (cd2) to (c);
            \draw (cd2) to (d);
            \draw (cd2) to (cd1);

            \begin{scope}[on background layer, scale=1.5]
                \draw[fill=red!20, draw=none] \convexpath{ab1,b,z2,z1,a}{3.5pt};
                \draw[fill=red!20, draw=none] \convexpath{d,cd2,cd1}{3.5pt};
                \draw[fill=blue!20, draw=none] \convexpath{e,a'}{3.5pt};
                \draw[fill=blue!20, draw=none] \convexpath{b',b''}{3.5pt};
                \node[fit= (c), inner sep=0.5pt, draw=none, fill=blue!20] {};
                \node[fit= (ab2), inner sep=0.5pt, draw=none, fill=blue!20] {};
                \node[fit= (c'), inner sep=0.5pt, draw=none, fill=red!20] {};
                \node[fit= (c''), inner sep=0.5pt, draw=none, fill=red!20] {};
            \end{scope}
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{A monopolar partition of $G$.}
        \label{fig:monopolar_partition_chordal_4}
    \end{figure}

    \paragraph{Case 2.1.2: $N(c)\setminus \l{x}$ is not a cluster.} By a
    previous argument, we have that $G[N(x)\setminus \l{c}]$ is a cluster. Let
    $w$, $y$ and $z$ be vertices in $N(c)\setminus \l{x}$ such that $\p{w,y,z}$
    is an induced copy of $P_3$. We claim that $N_{abc}=\l{x}$. Indeed, if there
    is an $x'\in N_{abc}$, since $N_{abc}$ is a clique, we have that $x$ is
    adjacent to $x'$. Furthermore, $w$, $y$ and $z$ are not adjacent to neither
    $x$ nor $x'$, so $\l{c,w,x,x',y,z}$ induces a copy of $H_4$ (see
    \Cref{fig:3_bag_8}). Then, $N_{abc}=\l{x}$.

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
            distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
            \node[Label] (v) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (d) {};
            \node (z1) at (-0.5,0) {};
            \node (z2) at (0.5,0) {};
            \node (w) at (0,-1.75) {};
            \node (y) at (-0.75,-1.75) {};

            \draw[lightgray] (z1) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (z1) to (a);
            \draw[lightgray] (z2) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (a) to (b);
            \draw[lightgray] (z2) to (a);
            \draw (z2) to (z1);
            \draw (z2) to (c);
            \draw (z1) to (c);
            \draw (c) to (d);
            \draw (c) to (w);
            \draw (d) to (w);
            \draw (y) to (w);
            \draw (y) to (c);
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{An induced copy of $H_4$ given that $N_{abc}$ is not trivial.}
        \label{fig:3_bag_8}
    \end{figure}

    Notice $N_a$ is empty, otherwise, $a'\in N_a$ is a vertex adjacent to $x$,
    so $\p{a',a,b,}$ is an induced copy of $P_3$ in $G[N(x)\setminus\l{c}]$, a
    contradiction to such a graph being a cluster. Analogously, $N_b$ is empty.
    
    We also claim that $N_{ab}$ is a clique. If that is not the case, then there
    are $v$ and $v'$ nonadajcent vertices in $N_{ab}$. Recall that $v$ and $v'$
    are adjacent to $x$. Thus, $\p{v,a,v'}$ is an induced copy of $P_3$ in
    $G[N(x)\setminus\l{c}]$, once more contradicting that such a graph is a
    cluster. Thus, $N_{ab}$ is a clique.

    Similarly, observe that $N_\varnothing$ is anticomplete to $N_{ab}$,
    otherwise a vertex $v'\in N_\varnothing$ adjacent to a vertex $v\in N_{ab}$
    yields an induced copy of $P_3$, $\p{v',v,a}$, in $G[N(x)\setminus\l{c}]$, a
    contradiction to such a graph being a cluster. Therefore, $N_\varnothing$ is
    anticomplete to $N_{ab}$.

    Finally, we assert that $G[N_\varnothing]$ is a cluster. Indeed, if that is
    not the case, there are vertices $v$, $v'$ and $v''$ in $N_\varnothing$ such
    that $\p{v,v',v''}$ is an induced copy of a $P_3$. Since all of them are
    adajcent to $x$, it follows that $\l{a,b,v,v',v'',x}$ induces a copy of
    $H_4$ (see \Cref{fig:3_bag_9}).

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
                    distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
                    \node (v) {};
                    \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (a) {};
                    \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (b) {};
                    \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (c) {};
                    \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v, lightgray] (d) {};
                    \node (x) at (-30:1) {};
                    \node (y) at (0:1) {};
                    \node (z) at (30:1) {};
                    \node[Label] at ($(0,0) - (y)$) {};

                    \draw[lightgray] (v) to (c);
                    \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
                    \draw (a) to (b);
                    \draw (v) to (b);
                    \draw (v) to (a);
                    \draw (v) to (x);
                    \draw (v) to (y);
                    \draw (v) to (z);
                    \draw (x) to (y);
                    \draw (z) to (y);
                \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{An induced copy of $H_4$ given that $G[N_\varnothing]$ is not a
        cluster.}
        \label{fig:3_bag_9}
    \end{figure}

    Therefore,
    \[
        \p{S_c \cup \l{x} , K_c\cup\l{c} \cup N_{ab}\cup\l{a,b} \cup
        N_\varnothing}
    \]
    is a monopolar partition of $G$ (see
    \Cref{fig:monopolar_partition_chordal_5}).

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
                    distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
                    \node (v) {};
                    \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (a) {};
                    \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (b) {};
                    \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (c) {};
                    \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of v] (d) {};
                    \node (v1) at ($(v) - (1,0)$) {};
                    \node (v2) at ($(v1) - (30:0.75)$) {};
                    \node (v3) at ($(v1) - (-30:0.75)$) {};
                    \node (w1) at ($(v) + (1,0)$) {};
                    \node (w2) at ($(w1) + (30:0.75)$) {};
                    \node (w3) at ($(w1) + (-30:0.75)$) {};
                    \node (ab1) at (-0.5,1.75) {};
                    \node (ab2) at (0.5,1.75) {};
                    \node (c') at ($(c) + (195:0.75)$) {};
                    \node (c'') at ($(c) + (165:0.75)$) {};

                    \begin{scope}[shift=($(c) - (90:0.75)$), rotate around={15:(c)}]
                        \foreach \i in {0,...,3}
                        \node (\i)  at ({(360/5)*(\i+1) + 90}:0.75){};
                    
                        \foreach \i in {0,...,3}{
                            \foreach \j in {1,...,3}{
                                \draw let \n1={int(mod(\i+\j,4))} in (\i) to (\n1);
                            }
                        }
                    \end{scope}

                    \draw (v) to (c);
                    \draw (c) to (d);
                    \draw (a) to (b);
                    \draw (v) to (b);
                    \draw (v) to (a);
                    \draw (v) to (v1);
                    \draw (v) to (v2);
                    \draw (v) to (v3);
                    \draw (v1) to (v2);
                    \draw (v1) to (v3);
                    \draw (v3) to (v2);
                    \draw (v) to (w1);
                    \draw (v) to (w2);
                    \draw (v) to (w3);
                    \draw (w1) to (w2);
                    \draw (w1) to (w3);
                    \draw (w3) to (w2);
                    \draw (ab1) to (ab2);
                    \draw (ab1) to (a);
                    \draw (ab1) to (b);
                    \draw (ab1) to (v);
                    \draw (ab2) to (v);
                    \draw (ab2) to (a);
                    \draw (ab2) to (b);
                    \draw (0) to (c');
                    \draw (c') to (c);
                    \draw (c'') to (c);
                    \draw (c) to (0);
                    \draw (c) to (1);
                    \draw (c) to (2);
                    \draw (c) to (3);

                \begin{scope}[on background layer, scale=1.5]
                    \draw[fill=red!20, draw=none] \convexpath{v1,v2,v3}{3.5pt};
                    \draw[fill=red!20, draw=none] \convexpath{w1,w2,w3}{3.5pt};
                    \draw[fill=blue!20, draw=none] \convexpath{c',c''}{3.5pt};
                    \draw[fill=red!20, draw=none] \convexpath{a,ab1,ab2,b}{3.5pt};
                    \draw[fill=red!20, draw=none] \convexpath{c,3,2,1,0}{3.5pt};
                    \node[fit= (v), inner sep=0.5pt, draw=none, fill=blue!20] {};
                    \node[fit= (d), inner sep=0.5pt, draw=none, fill=blue!20] {};
                \end{scope}
                \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{A monopolar partition of $G$.}
        \label{fig:monopolar_partition_chordal_5}
    \end{figure}
    
    \paragraph{Case 2.2: All 3-bags are empty.} By
    \Cref{lem:chordal_connectedness}, there are adjacent vertices $x$ and $y$,
    such that $x$ is complete to the edge $ab$ and anticomplete to the edge
    $cd$, whereas $y$ is complete to the edge $cd$ and anticomplete to the edge
    $ab$. Observe $\l{a,b,y,c}$ induces a copy of $2K_2$ with $x$ adjacent to
    $a$, $b$, and $y$, so one can proceed as in the previous subcase (see
    \Cref{fig:chordal_final}).

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node distance=1cm,
            on grid, edge]
            \node[Label] (z) {};
            \node[above left=1cm and 0.75cm of z] (a) {};
            \node[above right=1cm and 0.75cm of z] (b) {};
            \node[below left=1cm and 0.75cm of z] (c) {};
            \node[below right=1cm and 0.75cm of z, lightgray] (d) {};
            \node (x) at (0,0.5) {};
            \node (y) at (0,-0.5){};

            \draw[lightgray] (c) to (d);
            \draw[lightgray] (y) to (d);
            \draw (a) to (b);
            \draw (x) to (a);
            \draw (y) to (c);
            \draw (x) to (y);
            \draw (x) to (b);
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{An induced copy of $2K_2$ with a vertex adjacent to three of its vertices.}
        \label{fig:chordal_final}
    \end{figure}

    At last, we conclude that $G$ is monopolar.

\end{proof}

\section{\texorpdfstring{$(P_5,\text{house})$}{(P5,house)}-free graphs with \texorpdfstring{$C_5$}{C5}}
\label{sec:c5}

\begin{proposition}
    \label{prop:partition_c5}
    Up to symmetry, there is only one monopolar partition of $C_5$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
    Let $(A,B)$ be a monopolar partition of $C= \p{a,b,c,d,e,a}$. Since $C$
    contains an induced copy of $P_3$, at least one vertex must be in the
    independent part, say $a\in A$. Thus, $b$ and $e$ are in $B$. Since
    $\p{b,c,d,e}$ is a $P_4$ and $c$ and $d$ are adjacent, exactly one between
    $c$ and $d$ is in $A$ and the other in $B$, say $c$ is in $A$. Therefore,
    $(\l{a,c},\l{b,d,e})$ is a monopolar partition of $C$, and all partitions of
    it are constructed in this way.

\end{proof}

\begin{figure}[htb!]
    \centering
    \parbox{0.8\linewidth}{
        \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node

    \caption{Some minimal obstructions for monopolarity in
    $(P_5,\text{house})$-free graphs containing an induced copy of $C_5$.}
    \label{fig:p5-house-obstruction-with-c5}
\end{figure}

\begin{proposition}
    \label{prop:minimal_obs_c5}
    The graphs depicted in \Cref{fig:p5-house-obstruction-with-c5} are minimal
    monopolar obstructions.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
    Suppose $H_8$ is monopolar. By \Cref{prop:partition_c5}, without loss of
    generality the monopolar partition induced on its induced copy of $C_5$ is
    $(\l{a,c},\l{b,d,e})$. Then, $u$, the universal vertex in $H_8$, cannot be
    in $A$ since $a$ is adjacent to $u$, and cannot be in $B$, since $\p{b,u,d}$
    is an induced copy of $P_3$. It follows that $H_8$ is not monopolar.
    Moreover, in \Cref{fig:minimal_h8} the monopolar partitions of the unique
    graphs obtained from deleting a vertex from $H_8$ (up to isomorphism) are
    shown.

    \begin{figure}[htb!]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}
            \matrix[column sep=0.5cm]{
                \node{
                    \begin{tikzpicture}[every node/.style={vertex}, node
                        distance=1cm, on grid, edge]
                        \foreach \i in {0,...,4}

                        \draw (0) to (1);
                        \draw (1) to (2);
                        \draw (2) to (3);
                        \draw (3) to (4);
                        \draw (0) to (4);
        
                        \begin{scope}[on background layer, scale=1.5]
                            \node [fit = (4), draw=none, fill=blue!20, inner sep =
                            0.5pt] {};
                            \node [fit = (1), draw=none, fill=blue!20, inner sep =
                            0.5pt] {};
                            \node [fit = (0), draw=none, fill=red!20, inner sep =
                            0.5pt] {};

                        \draw (1) to (5);
